\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\hat{=}\,\bigl(\frac{v(k_{x}-\mathring{\imath}k_{y})}{2}\pm\frac{v(k_{x}+\mathring{\imath}k_{y})}{2}\bigr)G(\mathbf{k},1,0,\omega)\evaluated{G(\mathbf{k},0,0,\omega)}_{\gamma=0}&m^{\prime}=0\end{cases}

(58)

When using Equation (
58
) in Equations (
56
) and (
57
), the derivatives with respect to
k_{x}
and
k_{y}
will operate on the self propulsion vertex, Equation (
17
). Their respective effect will render the expression for
\langle x(t)\cos(\phi(t))\rangle
identical to that of
\langle y(t)\sin(\phi(t))\rangle
. After simplifying Equation (
58
) and using the definiton of the bare propagator, Equation (
16
), we can write the cross-correlator as

\langle\mathbf{x}(t)\cdot\mathbf{e}(\phi(t))\rangle=\frac{v}{2}\int\text{\dj}\omega e^{-\mathring{\imath}\omega t}\biggl[\frac{2}{(-\mathring{\imath}\omega+D_{\phi}+\gamma+r)(-\mathring{\imath}\omega+r)}\biggr.\\
\biggl.+\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{\cos{(m\psi_0)\gamma^m}}{-\mathring{\imath}\omega+D_{\psi}m^{2}+r}\prod_{i=0}^{m}\frac{1}{-\mathring{\imath}\omega+D_{\phi}+D_{\psi}i^{2}+\gamma+r}\biggr].

(59)

Figure 6:
Position-orientation cross-correlator —
\langle\mathbf{x}(t)\cdot\mathbf{e}(\phi(t))\rangle
plotted as a function of time,
t
, for a range of initial reorientation angles
\psi_{0}
with
D_{\phi}=0,D_{\psi}=1,\gamma=1
. Symbols indicate numerical simulations, solid lines Equation (
61
).

Integrating and taking
r\to 0
we find

\langle\mathbf{x}(t)\cdot\mathbf{e}(\phi(t))\rangle=\frac{v}{D_{\phi}+\gamma}[1-e^{-(D_{\phi}+\gamma)t}]\\
+v\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\cos{(m\psi_0)}\gamma^{m}\biggl[e^{-D_{\psi}m^{2}t}\prod_{i=0}^{m}\frac{1}{D_{\phi}+D_{\psi}(i^{2}-m^{2})+\gamma}\biggr.\\
\biggl.-\sum_{\ell=0}^{m}\frac{e^{-(D_{\phi}+D_{\psi}\ell^{2}+\gamma)t}}{D_{\phi}+D_{\psi}(\ell^{2}-m^{2})+\gamma}\prod_{i=0,i\neq\ell}^{m}\frac{1}{D_{\psi}(i^{2}-\ell^{2})}\biggr]

(60)

As for Equation (
41
), we consider
t\to 0
, which should produce
\langle\mathbf{x}(t\to 0)\cdot\mathbf{e}(\phi(t\to 0))\rangle
= 0. This becomes obvious only after writing Equation (
60
) as

\langle\mathbf{x}(t)\cdot\mathbf{e}(\phi(t))\rangle=\frac{v}{D_{\phi}+\gamma}[1-e^{-(D_{\phi}+\gamma)t}]\\
+v\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\cos{(m\psi_0)}\gamma^{m}\biggl[e^{-D_{\psi}m^{2}t}\sum_{\ell=0}^{m}\frac{1-e^{-(D_{\phi}+D_{\psi}(\ell^{2}-m^{2})+\gamma)t}}{D_{\phi}+D_{\psi}(\ell^{2}-m^{2})+\gamma}\prod_{i=0,i\neq\ell}^{m}\frac{1}{D_{\psi}(i^{2}-\ell^{2})}\biggr],

(61)

which also recovers the cross-correlator for an active Brownian particle with additional tumbling by a uniform reorientation angle in the limit
D_{\psi}\to\infty
, making the second line in Equation (
61
) vanish. At late-times, the cross-correlator plateaus at
\lim_{t\to\infty}\langle\mathbf{x}(t)\cdot\mathbf{e}(\phi(t))\rangle=v/(D_{\phi}+\gamma)
, identical to an active Brownian particle with tumbling
[
2
]
as
\psi
fully decorrelates, Figure
6
.
The cross-correlator, Equations (
60
) and (
61
), is symmetric under a change of sign of initial
\psi_{0}
, i.e. a change in initial tumble direction. To capture the parity of chirality, we calculate the position-perpendicular orientation cross-correlator

\langle\mathbf{x}(t)\cdot\mathbf{e}^{\perp}(\phi(t))\rangle=\langle y(t)\cos{(\phi(t))\rangle- \langle x(t)\sin{(\phi(t))\rangle}},

(62)

an observable that is antisymmetric in initial
\psi_{0}
, as we demonstrate in the following. The two terms on the right of Equation (
62
) can be expressed as

\langle y(t)\cos{(\phi(t)}\rangle=\frac{\mathring{\imath}}{2}\int\text{\dj}\omega e^{-\mathring{\imath}\omega t}\sum_{m^{\prime}=-\infty}^{\infty}\evaluated{\partial_{k_y}}_{\mathbf{k}=\mathbf{0}}[\langle\chi_{-1,0}(\mathbf{k},\omega)\tilde{\chi}_{0,m^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})\rangle\\
+\langle\chi_{1,0}(\mathbf{k},\omega)\tilde{\chi}_{0,m^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})\rangle]e^{-\mathring{\imath}m^{\prime}\psi_{0}},

(63)

and

\langle x(t)\sin{(\phi(t))}\rangle=\frac{1}{2}\int\text{\dj}\omega e^{-\mathring{\imath}\omega t}\sum_{m^{\prime}=-\infty}^{\infty}\evaluated{\partial_{k_x}}_{\mathbf{k}=\mathbf{0}}[\langle\chi_{-1,0}(\mathbf{k},\omega)\tilde{\chi}_{0,m^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})\rangle\\
-\langle\chi_{1,0}(\mathbf{k},\omega)\tilde{\chi}_{0,m^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})\rangle]e^{-\mathring{\imath}m^{\prime}\psi_{0}}.

(64)

To evaluate and simplify Equations (
63
) and (
64
) we work along the same lines as above. Notably, the contribution from the incoming
\psi-
mode
m^{\prime}=0
vanishes as it corresponds to uniformly distributed initial reorientation angle and thus a lack of chirality. We finally arrive at

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{x}(t)\cdot\mathbf{e}^{\perp}(\phi(t))\rangle=-v\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\sin{(m\psi_0)}\gamma^{m}\biggl[e^{-D_{\psi}m^{2}t}\sum_{\ell=0}^{m}\frac{1-e^{-(D_{\phi}+D_{\psi}(\ell^{2}-m^{2})+\gamma)t}}{D_{\phi}+D_{\psi}(\ell^{2}-m^{2})+\gamma}\prod_{i=0,i\neq\ell}^{m}\frac{1}{D_{\psi}(i^{2}-\ell^{2})}\biggr].

(65)

Figure 7:
Position-perpendicular orientation cross-correlator —
\langle\mathbf{x(t)}\cdot\mathbf{e}^{\perp}(\phi(t))\rangle
plotted as a function of time,
t
, for a variety of initial reorientation angles
\psi_{0}
with
D_{\phi}=0,D_{\psi}=1,\gamma=1
. Symbols indicate numerical simulations, solid lines Equation (
65
).

In Figure
7
, we see that stronger correlations arise for
1/(D_{\phi}+\gamma)=\tau_{p}\ll t\ll\tau_{\psi}=1/D_{\psi}
, where tumbles occur before
\psi
fully decorrelates, resulting in correlated motion. In direct contrast to the position-orientation cross-correlator, Equations (
60
) and (
61
), Equation (
65
) displays antisymmetry in
\psi_{0}
, simply as
\sin(m\psi_0)
is odd. To leading order,
m=1
, the correlator, Equation (
65
), is negative for
\psi_{0}\in[0,\pi)
and positive otherwise, while it vanishes at large
t
due to the exponential
\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{-D_{\psi}m^{2}t}}{\operatorname{exp}(-D_{\psi}m^{2}t)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-D_{\psi}m^{2}t\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-D_{\psi}m^{2}t\right)}
, thus reflecting the transient nature of the chirality.

8
Conclusion

In the present work, we have derived a Doi-Peliti field theory to describe the dynamics of transient chiral active particles (TCAPs) in two-dimensions. This allowed us to calculate a range of observables that have in turn allowed us to study memory effects in the reorientation dynamics under this novel model. In particular, we were able to study the mean squared displacement, both averaged over initial reorientation angle
\psi_{0}
, Equation (
33
), and for a fixed
\psi_{0}
, Equation (
39
). We then calculated the reorientation angle probability density function, Equation (
46a
), which allowed us to quantify the probability of tumbling to the right (equivalently to the left) given initial reorientation angle
\psi_{0}
, Equation (
47b
). We finally turned to orientational moments, and were able to calculate the orientational autocorrelator, Equation (
54
), position-orientation cross-correlator, Equation (
61
), and position-perpendicular orientation cross correlator, Equation (
65
).

We found that averaging over initial
\psi_{0}
recovers the MSD of an ABP (or RnT)
[
6
]
with directional diffusivity that is the sum of the angular diffusion
D_{\phi}
and the tumble rate
\gamma
. This in turn allowed us to identify short-time translationally diffusive and ballistic behaviour, Equation (
34
), and long-time effective diffusion, Equation (
35
). By fixing initial
\psi_{0}
we found a dependence on the diffusion of the reorientation angle
D_{\psi}
, as well as a difference in the dependence on
D_{\phi}
and
\gamma
, where the latter appears as a prefactor
\gamma^{m}
which can be interpreted as the number of tumbles that occur. While the short- and long-time behaviour remain unaffected by
D_{\psi}
, we identify two intermediate parameter regimes with dynamically different behaviour, governed by the speeds at which
\psi
and
\phi
decorrelate. If
\psi
decorrelates faster than
\phi
, the value of
\psi_{0}
has no discernible effect on
\phi
and we recover effectively active Brownian or run-and-tumble behaviour, Equation (
42
). If
\psi
decorrelates slower than
\phi
, we see strong dependence on
\psi_{0}
, with transiently chiral dynamics, Equation (
43
).

The reorientaion angle probability density function takes on the expected form for diffusion on a ring
[
43
]
, Equation (
46a
), and allowed us to show that initially tumbling to the right (resp. left) increases the likelihood of subsequently tumbling to the right (resp. left),
P(R|\psi_{0})
, giving rise to transiently chiral behaviour Equation (
47b
). We also saw that the period over which a particle remains transiently chiral is governed by
D_{\psi}/\gamma
; for a right-tumbling particle,
\psi_{0}\in[0,\pi)
,
P(R|\psi_{0})
decreases to
1/2
as
D_{\psi}/\gamma
increases because
\psi
decorrelates faster than it is probed (i.e. when a tumble occurs), meaning subsequent tumbles are less likely to have the same chirality, Figure
4
. The orientational autocorrelator, Equation (
54
) and Figure
5
, displays exponentially decaying behaviour, with dependence on
\psi_{0}
based largely on how fast
\psi
decorrelates. The position-orientation cross-correlator, Equations (
60
) and (
61
), is comprised of the result for an active Brownian or run-and-tumble particle
[
2
]
with corrections that are invariant under a change of sign in
\psi_{0}
, Figure
6
. The position-perpedicular orientation cross-correlator, Equation (
65
), is anti-symmetric in a change of sign of
\psi_{0}
; if
0<\psi_{0}<\pi
, then
\langle\mathbf{x}\cdot\mathbf{e}^{\perp}\rangle>0
. Conversely, if
-\pi<\psi_{0}<0
, then
\langle\mathbf{x}\cdot\mathbf{e}^{\perp}\rangle<0
. The cross-correlator allows us to identify the chirality of the particle in the transiently chiral regime, Figure
7
.

The present work illustrates how one approaches a field theory in which the particle has additional degrees of freedom, and uses this framework to analyse the motion of such particles. The dynamics of
\psi
could be further explored and modified, for example allowing
\psi
to evolve in a harmonic potential which would display transient chirality depending on the diffusivity of
\psi
and the strength and position of the potential. This work also paves the way to explore the collective behaviour of such particles, with potential to lead to the emergence of global transiently chiral behaviour.
